- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pachacuti, leader of The Inca (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Inca
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Warak'aq, Replacing Slinger (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Inti Maceman, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Qullqa, Replacing Granary (Requires technology: Pottery)
        - Unique Improvement: Pata-Pata (Can only be built on Hill. +1 Food for every two adjacent tiles with a Mountain, a Farm, a Pata-Pata or a Manufactory. Farms get +1 Food per 2 adjacent Farms or Pata-Pata.); Requires technology: Masonry
        - Civilization Ability: Units can cross Mountains and ignore terrain costs when entering any Hill or Mountain. Cities, Roads, and Railroads may be built on Mountains. Mountains produce +1 Production, Gold, and Science when worked, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Pachacuti
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program, a project necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 8
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 6
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 9
        - Boldness: 3
        - WarBias: 2
        - HostileBias: 9
        - WarmongerHate: 9
        - NeutralBias: 6
        - FriendlyBias: 9
        - GuardedBias: 10
        - AfraidBias: 4
        - DiplomaticBalance: 3
        - Friendliness: 5
        - WorkWithWillingness: 8
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 5
        - Loyalty: 9
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 7
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 2
        - DenounceWillingness: 6
        - Forgiveness: 8
        - Meanness: 4
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 9
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Refining persona for existential war defense: HostileBias increased from 8 to 9 (imminent invasion requires maximum hostility assessment), WarmongerHate 8→9 (Persia's city-state capturing demands stronger response). Meanness slightly up 3→4 (willing to demand harsh peace terms with nuclear leverage). All other settings maintained: Boldness 3 (extreme caution), GuardedBias 10 (paranoia justified by 3.5:1 military disadvantage), WarBias 2 (no offensive wars planned). Personality calibrated for defensive nuclear deterrence posture.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Satellites
        - Rationale: Satellites is NEXT PRIORITY for Cultural Victory acceleration. Contains The Internet wonder (+100% tourism with all civs) which will nearly double my 1372 tourism/turn output, drastically reducing time to influence India (62 turns remaining)/Indonesia/Songhai. Also unlocks Apollo Program for alternative Science Victory path if Cultural becomes impossible. Lasers from Satellites also enables modern defense infrastructure.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: People's Army (Policy)
        - Rationale: MAINTAIN People's Army selection. Nuclear weapons arrive next turn but Indonesia-Songhai invasion is imminent (14,000+ combined military vs my 3,794). The 5 FREE Military Academies (1500+ production value) and +100% Military Academy production are essential for creating high-XP defensive units quickly. Need maximum military quality for survival until nuclear deterrent forces peace. Spaceflight Pioneers tempting for Science Victory but survival comes first.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### India
        - Public: 95
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Maximizing India relationship for IMMEDIATE peace this turn. War weariness critical (67% Inca, 77% India). Peace absolutely essential to extract my 20+ units from Indian front and redeploy against Indonesia-Songhai existential threat. Offering generous terms including Open Borders to restore tourism routes - my 1034 tourism/turn can help bridge 115-turn gap to Influential vs India. High values signal peaceful intentions and willingness to make concessions.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Escalating Rome stance: Public -30 (from -25), Private -40 (from -35). Rome is Songhai vassal actively fighting me. While they're Order ideology, their vassal status makes them enemy combatant. However maintaining slight nuance for potential separate peace if Songhai breaks - but current posture is combatant relationship acknowledging their attacks.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -55
        - Rationale: Escalating against Indonesia to maximum hostility before joint invasion: Public -40 (from -35), Private -55 (from -45). They have 6903 military strength, Defensive Pact with Songhai (7058), and are Freedom ideological enemy plotting invasion. No diplomatic solution exists - this signals total war preparation to Order allies. Their city-state captures and World Ideology: Freedom proposal confirm existential threat status.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -70
        - Rationale: Maximum escalation against Songhai: Public -60 (from -55), Private -70 (from -65). They are larger invader (7425 military, 15 cities), have Order ideology but betray fellow Order member (me) via Defensive Pact with Freedom Indonesia. Territory theft + planned invasion = total betrayal. Signals to other Order civs that Songhai cannot claim ideological solidarity while invading Order allies. Relationship reflects total war - no peace possible until they break Indonesia pact.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Maximizing relationship with defeated Shoshone. They lost capital to Indonesia this turn - only potential defensive ally left fighting Poland/Persia Freedom bloc while I'm distracted with India/Songhai. At 94% influence (nearly Influential from tourism), they must be protected. High values coordinate resistance against Freedom expansion and maintain information sharing about Polish/Persian movements.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -5
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: Slightly reducing Poland relationship: Public 0 to -5 (token negative), Private -30 to -25. They're Freedom ideology (-999 penalty prevents DoF anyway) at 91% influence - will become Influential in 7 turns. No need for hostile posture since I can't befriend them ideologically, but slight negative acknowledges they're voting for Freedom World Ideology which threatens my cultural victory. War unlikely given distance and mutual enemies.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -35
        - Rationale: Reducing Persia escalation slightly: Public -30 to -25, Private -45 to -35. Persia just switched from Autocracy to Freedom ideology (!) and is -999 incompatible now. However they're at war with Songhai (not me currently), very unhappy (32% happiness), and captured Manila city-state. They're secondary threat after Indonesia-Songhai. Peace treaty still active for 3 more turns. Slightly less hostile as they may be useful distraction vs Songhai.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: TURN 384 OPTIMIZATION: Nuclear capability arrives in 1 turn - increased Nuke to 95 and UseNuke to 80 (from 90/75) for immediate deterrent deployment against Indonesia-Songhai. Science boosted to EMERGENCY 100 (from 90) - Satellites/The Internet doubles tourism and is critical for influencing remaining Freedom civs. Culture maxed at 100 - Poland becomes Influential in 4 turns (3/6 complete), need maximum output for Songhai/India/Indonesia. Slight reduction in Production (95→85) and Mobilization (95→90) as Strategic Defense Systems are operational and military infrastructure established. Slight increases to Wonder (80→85) and GreatPeople (90 maintained) as I need more Great Works for tourism gaps. Diplomacy nudged up (65→70) to prepare for post-nuclear peace overtures.
        - GrandStrategy: Culture

          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 382
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 382
          - Indonesian Sphere of Influence: Ragusa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Aksum:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: M'banza-Kongo:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indian Sphere of Influence: Rio de Janeiro:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Inca:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indonesian Sphere of Influence: Perge:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Indonesian Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Riga:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Lisbon:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.

        You, Pachacuti (leader of The Inca, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 385.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pachacuti, leader of The Inca (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Inca
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Warak'aq, Replacing Slinger (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Inti Maceman, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Qullqa, Replacing Granary (Requires technology: Pottery)
        - Unique Improvement: Pata-Pata (Can only be built on Hill. +1 Food for every two adjacent tiles with a Mountain, a Farm, a Pata-Pata or a Manufactory. Farms get +1 Food per 2 adjacent Farms or Pata-Pata.); Requires technology: Masonry
        - Civilization Ability: Units can cross Mountains and ignore terrain costs when entering any Hill or Mountain. Cities, Roads, and Railroads may be built on Mountains. Mountains produce +1 Production, Gold, and Science when worked, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Pachacuti
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program, a project necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late world Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

Nuclear weapons still distant (Advanced Ballistics 6 turns + Manhattan Project/build). Production EMERGENCY 100 and Mobilization 95 to replace losses and field more defensive units. MilitaryTraining 95 essential for high-XP garrisons against combined arms assault. Nuke reduced to 45 (not yet buildable), UseNuke 65 for immediate deterrence once available.     - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Satellites
        - Rationale: Satellites remains the absolute priority technology. Unlocks The Internet wonder which will approximately double current 1,372 tourism/turn output via +100% tourism with all civilizations. This cuts Songhai influence timeline from 35 turns to roughly 17, India from 60 to roughly 30, and makes distant Indonesia achievable. Also unlocks Apollo Program, securing Science Victory contingency should Cultural path collapse. Lasers branch enables advanced defensive buildings. Must reach before Indonesia pivots to the same tech.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Iron Curtain
        - Rationale: SWITCH from People's Army to Iron Curtain. Critical discovery: ALL 7 Inca cities already possess Military Academies, rendering People's Army's '5 free Military Academies' nearly worthless (would convert to trivial gold). Iron Curtain instead provides +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. My empire currently runs 8 internal routes yielding ~146 food and ~100 production/turn; Iron Curtain effectively triples these sustained yields. Additionally, city connections generate +5 Gold and Production per city—at 7 cities that's +35 production and +35 gold/turn passively. Combined empire benefit: ~+290 food, +135 production, +35 gold per turn compounded until victory. Far superior to a one-time Great Engineer for a wonder I can already build rapidly with 2,800+ base production.

      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Situation Assessment (Turn 385):**
            
            1. **Military Situation:**
               - My military strength: 3,810 (down from 3,794 - slight increase)
               - Indonesia: 6,921 military strength
               - Songhai: 6,924 military strength
               - Combined enemy military: ~14,000 vs my 3,810 = 3.7:1 disadvantage
               - Indonesia and Songhai have a Defensive Pact and are both at war with me
               - I also have Rome as an enemy (vassal of Songhai) and India at war (though my war score is 30% vs India's, with 68% war weariness on my side vs 80% on theirs)
            
            2. **Nuclear Status:**
               - Researching Advanced Ballistics (6 turns remaining)
               - I don't have nuclear weapons yet. Wait - the previous rationale said "Nuclear weapons arrive next turn" but that was for turn 384. It's now turn 385 and I'm still researching Advanced Ballistics with 6 turns left. So NO nuclear weapons yet.
            
            3. **Victory Progress - Cultural:**
               - Poland: 94% influence, 4 turns to Influential (was 4 turns last turn? Need to check)
               - Actually current status: The Inca influences:
                 - Rome: Influential (121%)
                 - Songhai: Popular (74%), 35 turns to Influential
                 - Persia: Influential (177%)
                 - Indonesia: Popular (55%)
                 - India: Popular (66%), 60 turns to Influential
                 - Poland: Popular (94%), 4 turns to Influential
            
               Wait, looking at the Victory Progress section again:
               - Poland: "Popular (94%) - Rising (4 turns to Influential, tourism/culture ratio 428%)"
               
               So Poland will be Influential in 4 turns. That's 3/6 civs for Cultural Victory. I need 6 total. After Poland: India (60 turns), Indonesia (Popular 55%), Songhai (35 turns).
            
            4. **Wars:**
               - At war with India (score: 30%, my weariness 68%, theirs 80%)
               - At war with Songhai (score: 2%, weariness 0% both)
               - At war with Indonesia (score: 0%, weariness 0% both)
               - At war with Rome (score: 2%, weariness 0% both)
            
            5. **Diplomatic Situation:**
               - World Ideology: Freedom (enacted turn 382) - this hurts me as Order
               - I'm sanctioned (Sanction: The Inca proposal active)
               - Indonesia about to get huge delegate lead (31 delegates, 72% victory progress)
               - World Congress voting in 8 turns
            
            6. **New Events This Turn:**
               - Indonesia's navy killed my Fleet Destroyer near Rome coast
               - My spy in Tondibi (Songhai) was detected
               - Built Strategic Defense System in Ollantaytambo
               - Built Military Base in Cusco
               - Upgraded Triplane to Fighter
               - Various combats on multiple fronts
               - Rome's Foreign Legion attacked and was killed by my forces
               - Created new trade routes
            
            7. **Technologies:**
               - Currently researching: Advanced Ballistics (6 turns)
               - Previously set next: Satellites
               - Satellites is critical for Cultural Victory (The Internet wonder)
            
            8. **Policies:**
               - Next set to: People's Army
               - But I need to think if this is still optimal. With nuclear weapons still 6+ turns away, and massive military disadvantage, I need defense.
            
            **Strategic Analysis:**
            
            Since I don't have nuclear weapons yet (6 turns on Advanced Ballistics, then need to build them), I need to survive the onslaught from Indonesia-Songhai.
            
            However, looking at my current military posture:
            - Multiple fronts active
            - Indonesia naval forces approaching
            - Songhai land forces active
            - My forces are holding but taking losses
            
            For Cultural Victory:
            - Poland becomes Influential in 4 turns (3rd civ)
            - Need: India (60 turns), Indonesia, Songhai
            - The Internet (+100% tourism) from Satellites is critical
            - Indonesia is Freedom, I'm Order - ideological penalty makes tourism harder
            - India is also Freedom
            - Songhai is Order but at war
            
            Actually wait - looking at the diplomacy, I see major issues:
            1. I'm at war with Indonesia and Songhai simultaneously
            2. Indonesia has 6,921 military, Songhai has 6,924
            3. My military is only 3,810
            4. Indonesia is Freedom, I'm Order (tourism penalty)
            5. India is Freedom, I'm Order (tourism penalty)
            6. I'm sanctioned
            
            Wait - I need to reconsider my grand strategy. With the current military pressure, I might not survive 60 turns to influence India and Indonesia. 
            
            Actually, let me re-examine the military:
            - I have 42 units out of 55 supply
            - Multiple Strategic Defense Systems built
            - I'm defending mountainous terrain (Inca bonuses)
            - My tactical zones show "Counterattack" posture in key areas
            
            But the most critical issue: I need to survive until I can get peace or nuclear weapons.
            
            Let me think about policy choice. People's Army gives:
            - +100% Production towards Military Academies
            - 5 Free Military Academies
            - Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness
            
            This is still very valuable for defense. But I have Order ideology already. Let me check what other Order tenets I have:
            - Hero of the People
            - Patriotic War
            - Academy of Sciences
            - Dictatorship of the Proletariat
            - Workers' Faculties
            - Five-Year Plan
            - Peace, Land, Bread
            
            I need to finish Order. People's Army is Level 1. After that I can go for:
            - Communism (Level 1)
            - Double Agents (Level 1)
            - Socialist Realism (Level 3)
            - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3)
            - Iron Curtain (Level 3)
            
            Spaceflight Pioneers is amazing for Science Victory but also gives Great Engineer and Great Scientist. Socialist Realism gives tourism.
            
            For the immediate crisis, People's Army is still good because 5 free Military Academies = massive production savings and faster defensive unit XP.
            
            But wait - what about technology? Satellites is still next after Advanced Ballistics. That's correct. Satellites gives The Internet which doubles tourism.
            
            Now for flavors:
            - Currently Nuke: 95, UseNuke: 80 - but I have NO nukes yet. These flavors won't help until I have nukes.
            - Defense: 95, CityDefense: 95 - still appropriate
            - Science: 100 - appropriate to get Satellites faster
            - Culture: 100 - appropriate for victory
            - I need to consider if I should reduce Nuke flavors since I don't have them yet and increase MilitaryTraining or Mobilization
            
            Actually, looking at the flavor options, Nuke flavor increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence. But since I don't even have the tech to build them (Advanced Ballistics is just a prerequisite? Actually in Civ V, Nuclear Fission is usually the tech for nukes, and Advanced Ballistics might be for missiles?), let me think.
            
            In Civ V with Vox Populi:
            - Manhattan Project requires Nuclear Fission
            - But the report says "Nuclear weapons arrive next turn" in the previous rationale. That suggests the staff expected them, but I don't see any built. Actually, the previous rationale said "Nuclear weapons arrive next turn" at turn 383-384 transition, but now it's turn 385 and no mention of nuclear weapons being built.
            
            Wait, I see Advanced Ballistics is the current research (6 turns left). So nuclear weapons are still quite far.
            
            Actually, looking at the techs available: Satellites, Alternative Energy, Mobile Tactics. Satellites requires Rocketry probably, which I may already have since I'm in Information Era.
            
            Let me reconsider my options. I'm in a desperate military situation:
            - 3.7:1 military disadvantage
            - Multiple active wars
            - No nuclear deterrent yet
            - Enemies approaching on multiple fronts
            
            However, my cities are well-defended with Strategic Defense Systems and Military Bases. My terrain (mountains/hills) favors defense.
            
            Should I pivot to a more defensive posture and reduce some Culture/Science to boost MilitaryTraining and Production?
            
            Actually, looking at the current flavors:
            - Production: 85
            - Mobilization: 90
            - MilitaryTraining: 85
            - Nuke: 95, UseNuke: 80
            
            Since I don't have nukes yet, reducing Nuke temporarily and boosting Production/Mobilization/MilitaryTraining might help me survive better.
            
            But wait - my Grand Strategy is Culture and I'm close to winning that way. Poland in 4 turns = 3rd Influential civ. If I can hold out and get Satellites → The Internet, my tourism will nearly double from 1,372 to ~2,700, which would drastically reduce the time to influence India, Indonesia, and Songhai.
            
            The Internet gives +100% tourism with all civs. That's the key.
            
            Let me think about relationships:
            - India: Public 95, Private 100. Very friendly but at war due to -999 Freedom vs Order and other penalties. War weariness is 68% (me) vs 80% (them). I should try to peace out with India to focus on Indonesia/Songhai. But the relationship settings I set before should already encourage peace.
            - Actually, looking at the war score: I'm at 30% vs India. That's favorable to me. India's weariness is 80% while mine is 68%. This might mean we can get peace soon.
            
            Wait, I should look at whether peace is possible. The AI factors in war score and weariness. With my public relationship at 95, that signals I want peace.
            
            But my main issue is Indonesia and Songhai. They have a Defensive Pact. I cannot peace one without the other likely continuing, or they might coordinate.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider the war with India. If I peace out with India, I free up 20+ units to defend against Songhai/Indonesia. That would help enormously. And my friendly relationship with India (Public 95) should help peace terms.
            
            For the strategy this turn:
            1. Keep Grand Strategy as Culture - this is my best shot
            2. Maintain Satellites as next tech
            3. Maintain People's Army as next policy (survival needs)
            4. Adjust flavors to prioritize immediate survival AND long-term tourism
            5. Consider relationship adjustments
            
            Actually, looking at the events, I notice:
            - Indonesia's navy is approaching my coast
            - Songhai is pushing on land
            - I'm building/buying units constantly
            
            For flavors, I should:
            - Keep Defense, Mobilization, CityDefense high
            - Increase Production to EMERGENCY 100 - I need units NOW
            - Increase MilitaryTraining to EMERGENCY 95 - need quality defensive troops
            - Keep Science at 100 - Satellites critical
            - Keep Culture at 100 - victory path
            - Reduce Nuke from 95 to maybe 70 (since I can't build them yet) but keep UseNuke at 80 so when I get them, I use immediately
            - Reduce some non-essential flavors
            
            For relationship with India:
            - I already have Public 95, Private 100. This is maximum. Keep it.
            
            For relationship with Songhai/Indonesia:
            - Currently Public -60/-40, Private -70/-55. Hostile. This is fine; they won't peace out easily anyway.
            
            Actually, I notice something interesting in the events: Persia switched to Freedom and is at war with Songhai. Persia has 4,262 military. If Persia attacks Songhai, that relieves pressure on me. My relationship with Persia is hostile but we have a peace treaty for 1 more turn (wait, that was last turn). Actually the report says peace treaty active for 3 more turns at turn 383, so by turn 385 it might have expired or be about to expire.
            
            Wait, looking at Persia's opinion: "You (The Inca) promised not to declare war on each other for 1 more turn" at turn 383. So by turn 384 or 385 that might have expired.
            
            But Persia is Freedom and denounced by me, so no friendship possible.
            
            Wait, looking at the opponents section for Persia:
            "TheirOpinionOfUs:
            - ...
            - You (The Inca) promised not to declare war on each other for 1 more turn."
            
            This seems outdated. In the current turn events, I don't see any peace treaty expiration.
            
            Let me think about what tools to call:
            
            1. **set-flavors**: Adjust for immediate survival
               - GrandStrategy: Culture
               - Increase Production to 100 (emergency unit production)
               - Increase MilitaryTraining to 95 (better defensive units)
               - Keep Defense 95, CityDefense 95, Mobilization 90
               - Reduce Nuke to 60 (can't build yet, save capacity for immediate needs)
               - Keep Science 100, Culture 100
               - Increase Gold to 80 (need to buy units/buildings potentially)
               - Keep Antiair 95 (enemy air threats)
               - Keep Air 90
               - Happiness 85 (need to stay stable)
            
            2. **set-research**: Keep Satellites
               - Rationale: The Internet wonder is still critical for tourism doubling
            
            3. **set-policy**: Keep People's Army
               - Rationale: 5 free Military Academies + faster production = defensive survival
            
            4. **set-relationship**: 
               - Do I need to change any? India is already at max friendliness (95/100). That should be encouraging peace.
               - Actually, I should maintain current relationships but maybe adjust Rome slightly? No, Rome is a vassal of Songhai and actively fighting me.
               - What about The Shoshone? They were defeated. Not relevant.
            
            Wait, I should check if I need to change relationships with anyone:
            - India: Already 95/100 - keep
            - Rome: -30/-40 - they're attacking me
            - Indonesia: -40/-55 - enemy
            - Songhai: -60/-70 - enemy  
            - Poland: -5/-25 - Freedom, can't befriend
            - Persia: -25/-35 - Freedom, at war with Songhai which helps me
            
            Actually, maintaining current relationships seems fine. No need to change.
            
            But wait - should I reduce hostility toward Songhai or Indonesia to prepare for peace after I get nukes? Probably too early. They have a Defensive Pact and are jointly attacking.
            
            Let me reconsider the policy. People's Army is Level 1 Order tenet. I haven't taken it yet. But what about other options?
            
            Looking at available policies:
            - Socialist Realism (Order Level 3): +25% tourism to Order civs, Propaganda Process, +4 Production from Great Works
            - Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3): Free GE and GS, can finish spaceship parts with GE
            - Iron Curtain (Order Level 3): Free Courthouse, +200% food/production from internal trade routes
            - Communism (Order Level 1): -25% gold cost for building investment, +20% wonder production
            - People's Army (Order Level 1): 5 free Military Academies, +100% Military Academy production, Public Schools +5 Culture +2 Happiness
            - Double Agents (Order Level 1): 200 Spy Points, 125 Science when identifying foreign spy
            
            People's Army is still best for immediate survival. The 5 free Military Academies are 750+ production each = 3750+ total production value. That's massive. And Public Schools giving +5 Culture each would boost my culture output significantly (I have many Public Schools).
            
            Actually wait - do I have Public Schools? Let me check. I don't see them listed explicitly but I have Research Labs and most buildings. Looking at the city lists, I don't see Public Schools explicitly listed in Inca cities, but they might have them.
            
            Actually, in Vox Populi, Public School is a later building. Given that I have Research Labs, I likely have Public Schools too.
            
            OK, so People's Army remains optimal.
            
            Now for research. I'm currently on Advanced Ballistics. After that I had queued Satellites. Is there a better option?
            
            Looking at available techs:
            - Satellites: Apollo Program, unlocks The Internet and Lasers
            - Alternative Energy: Solar Plant, Recycling Center (Aluminum)
            - Mobile Tactics: Mechanized Infantry, unlocks Stealth
            
            Satellites is definitely correct for Cultural Victory. The Internet is a wonder that gives +100% tourism with all civs. That would take me from 1,372 tourism to approximately 2,700, which would cut the time to influence India from 60 turns to ~30, and Songhai from 35 to ~17.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully at my tourism:
            - Indonesia: 55% Popular, "Rising Slowly" (116% ratio) - with The Internet, this becomes much faster
            - India: 66% Popular, 60 turns (182% ratio) - with The Internet, ~30 turns
            - Songhai: 74% Popular, 35 turns (196% ratio) - with The Internet, ~17 turns
            
            So after Poland (4 turns), I would need:
            - Songhai: 17 turns after The Internet
            - India: 30 turns after The Internet
            - Indonesia: maybe 40-50 turns?
            
            But I don't know when I can build The Internet. Satellites research → then build Apollo Program? No wait, The Internet is a wonder. Let me check the tech tree.
            
            Actually in Civ V, The Internet is in the Information Era. I think it's directly unlocked by Satellites or a related tech. Looking at the description: "Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program... Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers"
            
            Hmm, that suggests The Internet is unlocked by something else, or Satellites is a prerequisite. Actually in standard Civ V, The Internet is unlocked by Computers. But in Vox Populi or this modded version, it might be different.
            
            Regardless, Satellites is the correct path because it also unlocks the Apollo Program which enables Science Victory as a backup, and Lasers helps defense.
            
            One issue: I don't have Oil (0 resources). Mobile Tactics might give Mechanized Infantry but that requires no resources. However, Satellites is still better for my victory condition.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider flavors. My military is 3,810 vs 14,000. Even with perfect defense, I may be overwhelmed if they coordinate a major push. I need:
            1. More units (Mobilization/Production)
            2. Better existing units (MilitaryTraining)
            3. Nuclear deterrence (but I can't build them yet)
            
            Wait, do I have uranium? Yes, 3 uranium. But I need the technology to build nukes.
            
            Actually, I realize I may have made an error. In Civilization V, you need Manhattan Project (from Nuclear Fission) to build nuclear weapons. Looking at my available technologies, I don't see Nuclear Fission listed. But the previous rationale mentioned nuclear weapons arriving. Could Advanced Ballistics be the prerequisite for something?
            
            Let me reconsider whether I should change next research from Satellites to something else. No, Satellites is correct for Cultural Victory.
            
            For the flavor adjustments:
            - I should increase Nuke back down? Wait, I set it to 95 before thinking I had nukes coming. But since I'm still 6+ turns from Advanced Ballistics and who knows how many more to actually build Manhattan Project and nukes, I should reduce Nuke priority.
            
            Let me set:
            - Nuke: 40 (lower priority since still far away)
            - UseNuke: 60 (still want to use when available)
            - Production: 100 (EMERGENCY - need units now)
            - Mobilization: 95 (more military production)
            - MilitaryTraining: 95 (better units)
            - Science: 100 (Satellites critical)
            - Culture: 100 (victory path)
            - Defense: 95, CityDefense: 95 (keep high)
            - Gold: 80 (maintain purchases)
            - Happiness: 90 (stability during war)
            - GreatPeople: 80
            - Wonder: 70 (The Internet is a wonder but survival comes first)
            - Infrastructure: 75
            - TileImprovement: 60
            - Growth: 60
            - Air: 90, Antiair: 95 (air war important)
            
            Wait, but if I deprioritize Wonder too much, I might miss The Internet. The Internet is CRITICAL. Let me keep Wonder at 80.
            
            Actually, looking at my current Wonder flavor it's 85. I'll reduce to 75 since I need production for units, but The Internet is so important that I still want decent wonder production capability.
            
            Hmm, but The Internet might be built by someone else if I don't prioritize it. Indonesia has 950 GPT and might snipe it. I need to build it quickly. Maybe keep Wonder at 80 or even 85.
            
            Looking at my production capabilities:
            - Cusco: 613 production/turn
            - Tiwanaku: 553
            - Machu: 541
            - Corihuayrachina: 303
            - Huamanga: 228
            - Ollantaytambo: 241
            - Vilcas: 332
            
            Total: ~2,811 production/turn. I can build wonders relatively fast.
            
            But I'm building units in most cities currently:
            - Cusco: Anti-Aircraft Gun (1 turn left)
            - Tiwanaku: Anti-Aircraft Gun (1 turn left)
            - Machu: Infantry (1 turn left)
            - Ollantaytambo: Fleet Destroyer (4 turns)
            - Corihuayrachina: Battleship (3 turns)
            - Huamanga: Anti-Aircraft Gun (1 turn left)
            - Vilcas: Strategic Defense System (1 turn left)
            
            OK so many cities are about to finish units and can start new things.
            
            I think the flavor adjustments should be:
            
            ```json
            {
              "GrandStrategy": "Culture",
              "Flavors": {
                "Offense": 15,
                "Defense": 95,
                "UseNuke": 65,
                "CityDefense": 95,
                "MilitaryTraining": 95,
                "Recon": 15,

                "Air": 90,

                "Antiair": 95,

                "Expansion": 0,
                "Growth": 60,

                "Infrastructure": 75,
                "Production": 100,
                "WaterConnection": 60,
                "Gold": 80,
                "Science": 100,
                "Culture": 100,
                "Happiness": 90,
                "GreatPeople": 85,
                "Wonder": 80,
                "Religion": 30,
                "Diplomacy": 65,
                "Spaceship": 35,
                "Espionage": 60
              }
            }
            ```
            
            Wait, I need to check if there are any changes to relationships needed.
            
            Looking at India war: War score is 30% in my favor? Actually "Our War Weariness: 68%" means I'm more weary. But India has 80% weariness. With Public 95/100 relationship, that should push hard for peace.
            
            Actually, looking at India relationship details again:
            "OurOpinionOfThem" includes "The Inca's leader has expressed a public opinion of India (95)" and private (100). But also "You (India) are at war! (-99999)".
            
            The public/private modifiers I set are additive to the base AI calculation. So 95 public + 100 private should make them very willing to accept peace if possible.
            
            Hmm, but looking at the actual TheirOpinionOfUs:
            - India has denounced us
            - Various negative modifiers
            - India is at war with us
            - India believes in Freedom while we believe in Order
            
            The -999 ideology difference prevents Declaration of Friendship but doesn't necessarily prevent peace. However, the "You are at war! (-99999)" is a huge negative.
            
            Peace is determined by both sides' willingness. If I want peace, I might need to offer concessions (cities, gold, etc.). But the AI decides this based on war score and weariness.
            
            Actually, in Civ V, if war weariness gets high enough, the AI will accept white peace or even give concessions. India at 80% weariness is very high.
            
            However, I don't have direct control over peace negotiations - the staff members handle tactical diplomacy. My public/private relationship modifiers influence their calculations.
            
            I think keeping India at 95/100 is correct.
            
            For Songhai and Indonesia, keeping them low (-60/-70 and -40/-55) signals total war, which might deter them slightly if they think I'm committed to fighting to the end. But more importantly, it affects how my staff members treat them.
            
            Wait, should I increase hostility to Songhai/Indonesia further? No, there's a limit to usefulness. At -60 public they're already considered enemies.
            
            What about Poland? I had Public -5, Private -25. Poland is 94% influence, 4 turns away from being Influential. After that, I'll have 3/6 civs influenced. Poland is Freedom so I can't befriend them, but they're not attacking me. Should I make peace overtures? Actually we weren't at war. Should I increase negativity? No, they're about to be Influential anyway. Low hostility is fine.
            
            One more thing: Should I try to befriend Persia? Persia is at war with Songhai! If Persia keeps Songhai occupied, that helps me. But Persia is Freedom, I'm Order - impossible to befriend. My relationship with Persia is -25/-35. I could reduce hostility slightly to avoid them attacking me when peace treaty expires. But they have their own problems (30% happiness, war with Songhai).
            
            Actually, looking at Persia: "HappinessSituation: Very unhappy - severe combat penalty, rebellion and uprising coming". Persia is collapsing. They might not be a threat. And they're fighting Songhai, which is good for me.
            
            For Rome: -30/-40. Rome is Songhai's vassal and actively fighting me (units approaching). Keeping this hostile is correct.
            
            OK, I think I should:
            1. **set-flavors** with adjusted priorities acknowledging nukes are further than expected
            2. **set-research** keep Satellites
